You should know that after the Tathāgata’s extinction, those who can copy, uphold, read, recite, make offerings to, and expound it for others will be covered by the Tathāgata’s robe, and guarded in the mindfulness of present Buddhas of other directions. These persons possess great power of faith, power of resolve, and power of wholesome roots. You should know that these persons dwell together with the Tathāgata, and their heads are rubbed by the hand of the Tathāgata.

Bhaiṣajyarāja! In every place, whether expounded, read, recited, copied, or wherever copies of the scripture reside, one should erect a seven-jeweled stūpa, making it lofty, broad, and adorned; there is no need to place relics within it.

For what reason? Within it is already the complete body of the Tathāgata. To this stūpa, one should offer all flowers, incense, necklaces, silk canopies, banners, streamers, music, and hymns, making offerings, revering, honoring, and praising it. If people see this stūpa, bow down, and make offerings, you should know that they are all near to anuttarasamyaksambodhi.

Bhaiṣajyarāja! Many people, householders and renouncers, practice the Bodhisattva path; if they cannot see, hear, read, recite, copy, uphold, and make offerings to this Lotus Sūtra, you should know that these persons do not yet skillfully practice the Bodhisattva path. If they hear this scripture, they are able skillfully to practice the Bodhisattva path.

If there are wandering beings seeking the Buddha path who see or hear this Lotus Sūtra, and having heard it, believe, understand, receive, and uphold it, you should know that these persons draw near to anuttarasamyaksambodhi.

Bhaiṣajyarāja! Suppose a man is thirsty and in need of water, digging on an arid high plain to seek it. Still seeing dry earth, he knows that water is yet distant; applying his labor without ceasing, he progressively sees damp earth, and gradually reaches mud; his mind becomes certain: he knows that water must be near.

Bodhisattvas are also like this: if they have not heard, not understood, and not been able to cultivate this Lotus Sūtra, you should know that these persons are still far from anuttarasamyaksambodhi; if they hear, understand, contemplate, and cultivate it, they must know that they are near to anuttarasamyaksambodhi.

For what reason? All Bodhisattvas’ anuttarasamyaksambodhi belongs entirely to this scripture; this scripture opens the gate of expedient means and reveals the genuine mark. The treasury of this Lotus Sūtra is deep, firm, and remote, where none can reach; now the Buddha, teaching and accomplishing Bodhisattvas, opens and reveals it.

Bhaiṣajyarāja! If there are Bodhisattvas who, hearing this Lotus Sūtra, are startled, doubtful, and fearful, you should know that they are newly resolved Bodhisattvas; if Śrāvakas hearing this scripture are startled, doubtful, and fearful, you should know that they are persons of arrogant pride.

Bhaiṣajyarāja! If there are good men and good women who, after the Tathāgata’s extinction, wish to expound this Lotus Sūtra for the fourfold assembly, how should they expound it?

These good men and good women should enter the Tathāgata’s room, put on the Tathāgata’s robe, sit upon the Tathāgata’s seat, and only then expound this scripture widely for the fourfold assembly.

The Tathāgata’s room is the great compassionate mind toward all wandering beings; the Tathāgata’s robe is the gentle and patient mind; the Tathāgata’s seat is the emptiness of all dharmas. Dwelling peacefully within these, afterward with an unflagging mind, one should expound this Lotus Sūtra widely for Bodhisattvas and the fourfold assembly.

Bhaiṣajyarāja! In other lands, I will dispatch conjured persons to assemble audiences to hear the Dharma for them, and also dispatch conjured bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, and upāsikās to listen to their expounding. These conjured persons, hearing the Dharma, will believe and accept it, following without opposition. If the expounder of Dharma is in a quiet, leisure place, I will then dispatch devas, Naga kings, spirits, gandharvas, asuras, and the like to listen to their expounding.

Although I am in another land, from time to time I will cause the expounder of Dharma to see my body. If they forget a phrase or verse of this scripture, I will speak it for them, causing them to be fully restored.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

If one wishes to abandon all negligence,

One should listen to this scripture;

This scripture is difficult to hear,

And those who believe and accept it are also rare.

Like a person thirsty for water,

Digging upon an arid high plain,

Still seeing dry earth,

He knows water is still far away;

Gradually seeing damp earth and mud,

He knows with certainty that water is near.

Bhaiṣajyarāja, you should know!

Such persons as these,

Not hearing the Lotus Sūtra,

Are far removed from the Buddha’s wisdom;

If they hear this profound scripture,

They definitively understand the Śrāvaka Dharma.

This is the King of all scriptures;

Having heard it and contemplated it carefully,

You should know that such persons

Are near to the Buddha’s wisdom.

If a person expounds this scripture,

They should enter the Tathāgata’s room,

Put on the Tathāgata’s robe,

And sit upon the Tathāgata’s seat;

Dwelling amidst the assembly without fear,

Widely distinguishing and expounding it.

Great compassion is the room,

Gentle patience is the robe,

The emptiness of all dharmas is the seat;

Dwelling here, one expounds the Dharma.

When expounding this scripture,

If someone reviles and abuses with evil mouths,

Or attacks with knives, sticks, tiles, and stones,

Mindful of the Buddha, one should endure it.

In my thousands of billions of lands,

I manifest pure, firm bodies,

Throughout immeasurable billions of kalpas,

Expounding the Dharma for wandering beings.

If after my extinction,

There are those who can expound this scripture,

I will dispatch conjured fourfold assemblies,

Bhikṣus and bhikṣuṇīs,

And pure faithful men and women,

To make offerings to the Dharma teacher,

Guiding wandering beings,

Assembling them to hear the Dharma.

If people wish to inflict harm,

With knives, sticks, tiles, and stones,

I will dispatch transformed persons

To serve as guards for them.

If the expounder of Dharma,

Is alone in a quiet, leisure place,

Silent without the sound of people,

Reading and reciting this scripture,

I will then manifest for them

A pure, radiant body.

If they forget a chapter or verse,

I will speak it to make them fluent.

If a person possesses these virtues,

Whether expounding for the fourfold assembly,

Or reading and reciting the scripture in an empty place,

They will all see my body.

If a person is in an empty, quiet place,

I will dispatch devas, Naga kings,

Yakṣas, ghosts, and spirits,

To serve as audiences listening to the Dharma.

This person delights in expounding the Dharma,

Distinguishing without hindrance;

Because of the protective mindfulness of the Buddhas,

They can cause the great assembly to rejoice.

If one draws close to the Dharma teacher,

One swiftly attains the Bodhisattva path;

Following and learning under this master,

One attains beholding Buddhas like Ganges sands.

← Capítulo anteriorCapítulo siguiente →

Capítulo XI

La visión del stūpa de joyas

Este capítulo aún está en traducción al castellano; se muestra provisoriamente en inglés.

At that time, before the Buddha, there appeared a seven-jeweled stūpa, five hundred yojanas in height, two hundred and fifty yojanas in length and breadth, springing up from the earth and suspended in open space, adorned and decorated with various precious objects.

It had five thousand railings, thousands of millions of niches and chambers, adorned with countless banners and streamers, draped with jewel garlands, with tens of billions of jewel bells suspended above it. On all four sides poured forth the fragrance of tamālapatra-candana, pervading all worlds. Its banners and canopies were made of the seven treasures: gold, silver, lapis lazuli, mother-of-pearl, agate, pearls, and carnelian, rising as high as the palaces of the Four Heavenly Kings.

The devas of Trāyastriṃśa rained down heavenly māndārava flowers to make offerings to the jewel stūpa. The other devas, Naga kings, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kiṃnaras, mahoragas, humans, and non-humans, tens of billions of multitudes, with all flowers, incense, necklaces, banners, canopies, and music, made offerings to the jewel stūpa, revering, honoring, and praising it.

At that time, from within the jewel stūpa came forth a great voice, praising: “Excellent, excellent! Śākyamuni World-Honored One! Able, through equal great wisdom, to expound for the great assembly the Lotus Sūtra of the Wonderful Dharma, a teaching for Bodhisattvas and guarded in the mindfulness of the Buddhas. Thus it is, thus it is! Śākyamuni World-Honored One! All that you have expounded is the genuine reality.”

Then the fourfold assembly, seeing the great jewel stūpa suspended in open space and hearing the voice coming forth from within the stūpa, all attained Dharma joy, wondering at what they had never seen before. Rising from their seats, reverently joining palms, they withdrew to stand on one side.

At that time, there was a Bodhisattva Mahāsattva named Mahāpratibhāna, who, knowing the doubts in the minds of all devas, humans, and asuras of the world, addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! By what causal condition has this jewel stūpa sprung up from the earth, and why does this voice issue from within it?”

Then the Buddha addressed Mahāpratibhāna Bodhisattva: “Within this jewel stūpa is the complete body of the Tathāgata. Long ago in the past, in the eastern direction across immeasurable thousands of billions of incalculable worlds, there was a land named Ratnaviśuddha. In that land was a Buddha named Prabhūtaratna.

When that Buddha was practicing the Bodhisattva path, he made a great vow: ‘If, after I become a Buddha and pass into extinction, in lands of the ten directions there is a place where the Lotus Sūtra is expounded, my stūpa shrine, for the sake of hearing this scripture, will spring forth before it, bearing witness and praising: “Excellent!”’

After that Buddha accomplished the path, approaching extinction, amidst the great assembly of devas and humans he addressed the bhikṣus: ‘After my extinction, those who wish to make offerings to my complete body should erect a great stūpa.’ That Buddha, through the power of his spiritual vows, throughout the worlds of the ten directions, wherever the Lotus Sūtra is expounded, has his jewel stūpa spring forth before it, with his complete body within the stūpa, praising: ‘Excellent, excellent!’

Mahāpratibhāna! Now the stūpa of Prabhūtaratna Tathāgata, hearing the Lotus Sūtra expounded, has sprung up from the earth, praising: ‘Excellent, excellent!’”

Then Mahāpratibhāna Bodhisattva, through the spiritual power of the Tathāgata, addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! We wish to behold this Buddha’s body.”

The Buddha addressed Mahāpratibhāna Bodhisattva Mahāsattva: “This Prabhūtaratna Buddha has a deep and heavy vow: ‘When my jewel stūpa, for the sake of hearing the Lotus Sūtra, emerges before the Buddhas, if anyone wishes to display my body to the fourfold assembly, all the emanation Buddhas of that Buddha expounding the Dharma in worlds of the ten directions must gather together in one place; only then will my body appear.’

Mahāpratibhāna! All the emanation bodies of myself expounding the Dharma in worlds of the ten directions should now be assembled.”

Mahāpratibhāna addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! We also wish to behold the emanation Buddhas of the World-Honored One, to bow down and make offerings.”

Then the Buddha emitted a light from the white curl between his eyebrows, immediately revealing the Buddhas of lands equal to five hundred million billion inconceivable numbers of Ganges sands in the eastern direction. All those lands had crystal as their ground, adorned with jewel trees and precious garments, filled with countless thousands of billions of Bodhisattvas, with jewel curtains hung all around and jewel nets spread above.

The Buddhas of those lands expounded dharmas with great, subtle sounds, and immeasurable thousands of billions of Bodhisattvas were seen filling those lands, expounding the Dharma for the multitudes. The southern, western, northern directions, the four intermediate directions, zenith, and nadir, wherever the light of the white curl illuminated, were also like this.

At that time, the Buddhas of the ten directions each addressed their assemblies of Bodhisattvas: “Good children! I should now go to the Sahā world, to where Śākyamuni Buddha dwells, and make offerings to the jewel stūpa of Prabhūtaratna Tathāgata.”

Then the Sahā world was immediately transformed into purity: with lapis lazuli as its ground, adorned with jewel trees, golden cords bordering eight crossroads, free of villages, towns, cities, great oceans, rivers, streams, mountains, forests, and thickets. Great precious incense was burned, and māndārava flowers covered the ground; covered above with jewel nets and curtains, hanging with jewel bells. Only this assembled gathering was retained, while the devas and humans were relocated to other lands.

At that time, the Buddhas each brought a great Bodhisattva as an attendant, arrived in the Sahā world, and each went beneath a jewel tree. Each jewel tree was five hundred yojanas high, successively adorned with branches, leaves, flowers, and fruits. Beneath each jewel tree was a lion seat, five yojanas high, also decorated with great jewels.

Then the Buddhas each sat cross-legged upon these seats. Progressively filling the great three-thousand-fold worlds, the emanation bodies of Śākyamuni Buddha from just one direction were not yet exhausted.

Then Śākyamuni Buddha, wishing to accommodate all his emanation Buddhas, further transformed two hundred million billion inconceivable lands in each of the eight directions, causing them all to become pure, free of hells, hungry ghosts, animals, and asuras, and relocated the devas and humans to other lands.

The transformed lands also had lapis lazuli as their ground, adorned with jewel trees five hundred yojanas high, successively adorned with branches, leaves, flowers, and fruits, with jewel lion seats five yojanas high beneath each tree, adorned with various treasures. They were also free of great oceans, rivers, and mountain kings like Mount Mucilinda, Mahāmucilinda, the Iron Ring Mountains, the Great Iron Ring Mountains, and Mount Sumeru, united into a single Buddha land. The jewel ground was flat, covered with jeweled netting; banners and canopies hung, great precious incense burned, and heavenly jewel flowers covered the ground.

Śākyamuni Buddha, to provide seats for the arriving Buddhas, further transformed another two hundred million billion inconceivable lands in each of the eight directions, causing them all to become pure, free of hells, hungry ghosts, animals, and asuras, relocating devas and humans to other lands. The transformed lands were also of lapis lazuli ground, adorned with jewel trees five hundred yojanas high, with lion seats beneath each tree, united into a single Buddha land, covered with jewel curtains, banners, incense, and heavenly flowers.

Then the emanation bodies of Śākyamuni Buddha, Buddhas from lands numbering hundreds of thousands of millions of inconceivable numbers of Ganges sands in the eastern direction, each expounding the Dharma, gathered here. In this manner successively, the Buddhas of the ten directions all arrived and sat in the eight directions. At that time, in each direction, four hundred million billion inconceivable lands were filled with Buddhas, Tathāgatas.

Then the Buddhas, each seated upon a lion seat beneath a jewel tree, dispatched their attendants to greet Śākyamuni Buddha, each offering a handful of jewel flowers, saying: “Good son! Go to Mount Gṛdhrakūṭa to where Śākyamuni Buddha dwells, and speak as I say: ‘Are you free of sickness and affliction? Are your physical energies at ease, and are the assemblies of Bodhisattvas and Śrāvakas at peace?’ Scatter these jewel flowers to make offerings to the Buddha, and say: ‘That Buddha wishes to join in opening this jewel stūpa.’” All the Buddhas dispatched messengers in this manner.

Then Śākyamuni Buddha, seeing that all his emanation Buddhas had gathered, each seated upon a lion seat, and hearing that the Buddhas all wished to open the jewel stūpa together, immediately arose from his seat and stood in open space. The entire fourfold assembly rose, joined their palms, and single-mindedly gazed upon the Buddha.

Thereupon, Śākyamuni Buddha, with the finger of his right hand, opened the door of the seven-jeweled stūpa; it emitted a great sound, like unbarring a lock and throwing open a great city gate. Immediately the entire assembly beheld Prabhūtaratna Tathāgata seated upon the lion seat within the jewel stūpa, his complete body whole and unscattered, as if entering dhyāna meditation.

They also heard him say: “Excellent, excellent! Śākyamuni Buddha! Joyfully expound this Lotus Sūtra; I have come here for the sake of hearing this scripture.”

At that time, the fourfold assembly, seeing a Buddha who had passed into extinction immeasurable thousands of billions of kalpas ago speak such words, praised what they had never seen before, and scattered heaps of heavenly jewel flowers over Prabhūtaratna Buddha and Śākyamuni Buddha.

Then Prabhūtaratna Buddha, from within the jewel stūpa, offered half of his seat to Śākyamuni Buddha, saying: “Śākyamuni Buddha! Take this seat.” Immediately Śākyamuni Buddha entered the stūpa, sat upon half of the seat, and sat cross-legged.

Then the great assembly, seeing the two Tathāgatas sitting cross-legged upon the lion seat within the seven-jeweled stūpa, each thought: “The Buddha seats are lofty and distant; we pray the Tathāgata will use spiritual powers to cause us all to abide in open space.”

Immediately Śākyamuni Buddha, by spiritual power, drew the great assembly up into open space, and with a great voice universally addressed the fourfold assembly:

“Who can widely expound the Lotus Sūtra of the Wonderful Dharma in this Sahā world? Now is precisely the time. The Tathāgata will before long enter nirvāṇa; the Buddha wishes to entrust this Lotus Sūtra of the Wonderful Dharma so that it may endure.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

The Holy Lord, the World-Honored One,

Though long passed into extinction,

Within the jewel stūpa,

Still comes for the sake of the Dharma.

How could people

Not exert diligence for the Dharma?

This Buddha passed into extinction

Countless kalpas ago;

Everywhere he listens to the Dharma,

Because it is difficult to encounter.

That Buddha’s original vow:

“After my extinction,

Wherever I go,

It is constantly to hear the Dharma.”

Furthermore, my emanation

Immeasurable Buddhas,

Countless as Ganges sands,

Have come wishing to hear the Dharma,

And to behold the extinct

Prabhūtaratna Tathāgata.

Each abandoned their wonderful lands,

Disciples,

Devas, humans, Nagas, spirits,

And all articles of offering,

To cause the Dharma to abide long,

Therefore they arrived here.

To provide seats for the Buddhas,

By spiritual powers,

I relocated immeasurable multitudes,

Causing the lands to become pure.

The Buddhas each

Went beneath jewel trees,

Like pure ponds

Adorned with lotus flowers.

Beneath the jewel trees,

Upon the lion seats,

The Buddhas sat,

Radiant and adorned,

As in the dark of night,

A great torch is kindled.

Their bodies emitted wondrous fragrance,

Pervading lands of the ten directions;

Wandering beings receiving this incense,

Were filled with boundless joy.

Like a great wind

Blowing small tree branches;

Through this expedient means,

Causing the Dharma to abide long.

I tell the great assembly:

“After my extinction,

Who can protect, uphold,

Read, and recite this scripture?

Now before the Buddha,

Declare your own vows!

That Prabhūtaratna Buddha,

Though long passed into extinction,

Through his great vow,

Roars the lion’s roar.

Prabhūtaratna Tathāgata,

And my own body,

Along with the assembled transformed Buddhas,

You should understand this intent.

Children of the Buddhas,

Who can protect the Dharma?

You should make a great vow,

To cause it to abide long.

Those who are able to protect

The Dharma of this scripture,

Will thereby make offerings

To me and to Prabhūtaratna.

This Prabhūtaratna Buddha,

Dwelling within the jewel stūpa,

Constantly travels the ten directions

For the sake of this scripture;

One also makes offerings

To the arriving transformed Buddhas,

Who adorn and illuminate

All the worlds.

If one expounds this scripture,

One beholds me,

Prabhūtaratna Tathāgata,

And all the transformed Buddhas.

Good children!

Each contemplate carefully:

This is a difficult matter;

You should make a great vow.

Other scriptures,

Are numerous as the sands of the Ganges;

Even if expounding these,

It is not yet difficult.

If one picked up Mount Sumeru,

And hurled it to other directions,

Across countless Buddha lands,

That would not be difficult.

If with a toe,

One moved a great three-thousand-fold worlds,

Hurling it far to other countries,

That would not be difficult.

If standing on the Peak of Existence,

One expounded for the multitude

Immeasurable other scriptures,

That would not be difficult.

If after the Buddha’s extinction,

In an evil world,

One is able to expound this scripture,

That indeed is difficult!

Even if a person

Grasped empty space in their hand,

And wandered about with it,

That would not be difficult.

If after my extinction,

One copies and upholds it oneself,

Or causes others to copy it,

That indeed is difficult!

If placing the great earth

Upon a toenail,

One ascended to the Brahmā Heaven,

That would not be difficult.

After the Buddha’s extinction,

In an evil world,

To read this scripture for an instant,

That indeed is difficult!

Even if at the kalpa’s end-fire,

Carrying dry grass on one’s back,

One entered it without being burned,

That would not be difficult.

After my extinction,

If one upholds this scripture,

And speaks it for even one person,

That indeed is difficult!

If one upholds the eighty-four thousand

Treasuries of Dharma,

The twelve divisions of scriptures,

And expounds them to people,

Causing all listeners

To attain the six spiritual superknowledges;

Though able to do this,

It is not yet difficult.

After my extinction,

To listen to and accept this scripture,

And inquire into its meaning,

That indeed is difficult!

If a person expounds the Dharma,

Causing thousands of billions,

Immeasurable, countless

Ganges sands of wandering beings,

To attain arhatship,

Endowed with the six spiritual superknowledges;

Though bringing such benefit,

It is not yet difficult.

After my extinction,

If one can uphold

Such a scripture as this,

That indeed is difficult!

For the sake of the Buddha path,

In immeasurable lands,

From the beginning until now,

I have widely expounded scriptures;

Yet among them,

This scripture is foremost!

If one can uphold it,

One upholds the Buddha’s body.

Good children!

After my extinction,

Who can receive, uphold,

Read, and recite this scripture?

Now before the Buddha,

Declare your own vows!

This scripture is difficult to uphold;

If one upholds it for an instant,

I will rejoice,

And all Buddhas will also rejoice.

Such a person as this,

Is praised by all Buddhas.

This is heroism,

This is vigor,

This is named upholding precepts,

And practicing the dhūta austerities;

One will swiftly attain

The unexcelled Buddha path.

Able in future ages

To read and uphold this scripture,

One is a genuine Buddha-child,

Dwelling on the ground of pure goodness.

After the Buddha’s extinction,

Able to understand its meaning,

One is the eye of devas and humans

In the world.

In a terrifying world,

Able to expound it for an instant,

All devas and humans

Should make offerings to them.”

Capítulo XII

Devadatta

Este capítulo aún está en traducción al castellano; se muestra provisoriamente en inglés.

At that time, the Buddha addressed the Bodhisattvas, devas, humans, and the fourfold assembly: “In past immeasurable kalpas, I sought the Lotus Sūtra without weariness or fatigue. Throughout many kalpas, I constantly served as a king, making vows to seek unexcelled bodhi, my mind irreversible.

Wishing to fulfill the Six Pāramitās, I diligently practiced giving, without stinginess in heart: elephants, horses, the seven treasures, countries, cities, wives, children, male and female servants, attendants, head, eyes, marrow, brain, body, flesh, hands, and feet, not begrudging physical life.

At that time, the lifespan of people in the world was immeasurable. For the sake of the Dharma, I abandoned the royal throne, entrusted the government to the prince, struck the drum and proclaimed in the four directions, seeking the Dharma: ‘Who can expound the Great Vehicle for me? I will serve and attend upon them for the rest of my life!’

At that time, an ascetic came and addressed the king: ‘I possess the Great Vehicle, named the Lotus Sūtra of the Wonderful Dharma. If you do not disobey me, I will explain it to you.’

The king, hearing the ascetic’s words, rejoiced and leaped, immediately following the ascetic, supplying what was needed: picking fruits, drawing water, gathering firewood, preparing food, and even using his body as a bed and seat, body and mind unwearied, serving him at that time. Throughout a thousand years, for the sake of the Dharma, he diligently attended upon him, leaving him lacking in nothing.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

I recall past kalpas,

For the sake of seeking the great Dharma;

Although a king of the world,

Not craving the pleasures of the five desires.

Striking the bell to announce to the four directions:

“Who possesses the great Dharma?

If someone explains it for me,

I will become their servant.”

At that time there was the seer Asita,

Who came and addressed the great king:

“I have a subtle, sublime Dharma,

Rare in the world;

If you can practice it,

I will speak it for you.”

The king, hearing the seer’s words,

Gave rise to great joy in his heart,

Immediately following the seer,

Supplying whatever was needed,

Gathering firewood, fruits, and melons,

Respectfully presenting them in due season;

Because his heart cherished the wonderful Dharma,

Body and mind were free of weariness.

Universally for the sake of wandering beings,

Diligently seeking the great Dharma,

Not for his own body,

Nor for the pleasures of the five desires.

Thus as a great king,

Diligently seeking, he attained this Dharma,

And finally achieved Buddhahood;

Therefore I now speak it to you.

The Buddha addressed the bhikṣus: “The king at that time was myself; the aesthetic was Devadatta. Because Devadatta was a good friend, he caused me to be fully endowed with the Six Pāramitās, love, compassion, joy, equanimity, the thirty-two marks, the eighty subtle characteristics, purple-tinged golden color, the ten powers, the four fearlessnesses, the four means of embracing, and the eighteen distinct spiritual and path powers; accomplishing equal and right awakening, widely liberating wandering beings—all through the good friend Devadatta.”

He addressed the fourfold assembly: “Devadatta, after immeasurable kalpas have passed, will become a Buddha named Devarāja Tathāgata, Arhat, Samyaksambuddha, Vidyācaraṇasampanna, Sugata, Lokavid, Anuttara, Puruṣadamyasārathi, Śāstā Devamanuṣyāṇām, Buddha, Bhagavān. His world will be named Deva-satya.

At that time, Devarāja Buddha will dwell in the world for twenty intermediate kalpas, widely expounding the wonderful Dharma for wandering beings. Wandering beings equal to the sands of the Ganges will attain the arhat fruit; immeasurable wandering beings will generate the mind of Conditioned Awakening; wandering beings equal to the sands of the Ganges will generate the unexcelled path-mind, attaining the forbearance of non-arising dharmas and reaching non-retrogression.

When Devarāja Buddha enters parinirvāṇa, the Correct Dharma will abide in the world for twenty intermediate kalpas. A seven-jeweled stūpa will be erected for his complete-body relics, sixty yojanas in height, and forty yojanas in length and breadth. All devas and people will with various flowers, powdered incense, burning incense, paste incense, clothing, necklaces, banners, canopies, music, and hymns, bow down and make offerings to the seven-jeweled wondrous stūpa. Immeasurable wandering beings will attain the arhat fruit, immeasurable wandering beings will awaken to Pratyekabuddhahood, and inconceivable wandering beings will generate the bodhicitta, reaching non-retrogression.”

The Buddha addressed the bhikṣus: “In future ages, if there are good men and good women who, hearing the Devadatta Chapter of the Lotus Sūtra of the Wonderful Dharma, with pure minds believe and revere it without giving rise to doubts, they will not fall into hells, hungry ghosts, or animals, but will be born before Buddhas of the ten directions; wherever they are born, they will constantly hear this scripture. If born among humans or devas, they will experience superior, subtle joy; if born before a Buddha, they will be born by transformation from a lotus flower.”

At that time, an attendant Bodhisattva of Prabhūtaratna World-Honored One from below, named Prajñākūṭa, addressed Prabhūtaratna Buddha: “We should now return to our native land.”

Śākyamuni Buddha said to Prajñākūṭa: “Good son! Wait an instant. Here is a Bodhisattva named Mañjuśrī; you can meet with him and discuss the wonderful Dharma, and then return to your native land.”

Then Mañjuśrī, seated upon a thousand-petaled lotus as large as a carriage wheel—the accompanying Bodhisattvas also seated on jewel lotuses—spontaneously sprang forth from the great ocean, from the palace of Sāgara Naga King, suspended in open space, went to Mount Gṛdhrakūṭa, descended from the lotuses, went to where the Buddhas were, and bowed reverently with his head to the feet of the two World-Honored Ones. Having completed his reverence, he went to where Prajñākūṭa was, exchanged greetings, and withdrew to sit on one side.

Prajñākūṭa Bodhisattva asked Mañjuśrī: “Virtuous one, going to the Naga palace, how many wandering beings did you transform?”

Mañjuśrī said: “Their numbers are immeasurable, beyond calculation, cannot be proclaimed by the mouth, nor fathomed by the mind. Wait an instant, and there will be proof of itself.”

Before he had finished speaking, countless Bodhisattvas seated upon jewel lotuses sprang forth from the sea, went to Mount Gṛdhrakūṭa, and resided in open space. All these Bodhisattvas were transformed and liberated by Mañjuśrī, endowed with Bodhisattva conduct, all discussing the Six Pāramitās together. Those who had originally been Śrāvakas, while in open space, had spoken of Śrāvaka practices, but now were all cultivating the Great Vehicle meaning of emptiness.

Mañjuśrī said to Prajñākūṭa: “The teaching and transformation in the ocean were like this.”

Then Prajñākūṭa Bodhisattva praised in gāthās:

Great in wisdom, virtue, and valor,

Transforming and liberating immeasurable multitudes;

Now this great assembly,

And I have all seen it.

Proclaiming the meaning of the genuine mark,

Revealing and clarifying the One Vehicle Dharma,

Widely guiding all wandering beings,

Causing them swiftly to accomplish bodhi.

Mañjuśrī said: “Within the ocean, I constantly proclaimed only the Lotus Sūtra of the Wonderful Dharma.”

Prajñākūṭa asked Mañjuśrī: “This scripture is profound, subtle, and sublime, a treasure among scriptures, rare in the world. Are there wandering beings who, diligently exerting vigor and practicing this scripture, swiftly attain Buddhahood?”

Mañjuśrī said: “There is the daughter of Sāgara Naga King, just eight years old, whose wisdom faculties are sharp, skillfully knowing the faculties and karmic actions of wandering beings, having attained dhāraṇī, able fully to receive and uphold the profound, secret treasuries expounded by the Buddhas. Deeply entering dhyāna meditation, penetrating all dharmas, in an instant she generated the bodhicitta, attaining non-retrogression, with unhindered eloquence. Lovingly mindful of wandering beings as of infant children, her merits fully perfected, what she contemplates in mind and proclaims with mouth is subtle, sublime, and vast, compassionate, benevolent, yielding, her resolve gentle and elegant, able to arrive at bodhi.”

Prajñākūṭa Bodhisattva said: “I see that Śākyamuni Tathāgata, throughout immeasurable kalpas of difficult and bitter practices, accumulated merit and piled up virtue, seeking the path of bodhi without resting. Observing the great three-thousand-fold worlds, there is not even a spot as small as a mustard seed that is not a place where the Bodhisattva cast down body and life for the sake of wandering beings; and only afterward did he accomplish the path of bodhi. I do not believe that this girl in the space of an instant could accomplish right awakening.”

Before the discussion ended, the Naga king’s daughter suddenly appeared before them, bowed reverently with her head touching the ground, withdrew to stand on one side, and praised in gāthās:

Deeply penetrating the marks of transgressions and blessings,

Universally illuminating the ten directions;

The subtle, sublime, pure Dharma-body,

Endowed with the thirty-two marks,

With eighty subtle characteristics,

Adorning the Dharma-body.

Looked up to by devas and humans,

Revered by Nagas and spirits,

All classes of wandering beings,

None fail to venerate.

Furthermore, hearing of accomplishing bodhi,

Only the Buddha can bear witness;

I proclaim the Great Vehicle teaching,

Liberating suffering wandering beings.

Then Śāriputra said to the Naga girl: “You state that before long you will attain the unexcelled path; this matter is difficult to believe.

For what reason? A woman’s body is filthy and impure, not a vessel for Dharma; how could you attain unexcelled bodhi? The Buddha path is vast and distant; only after immeasurable kalpas of enduring hardship, piling up practices, and fully cultivating all pāramitās can it be accomplished.

Furthermore, a woman’s body has five obstacles: first, she cannot become a Brahmā King; second, Śakra; third, a Mara King; fourth, a wheel-turning sage king; fifth, the body of a Buddha. How could a woman’s body swiftly attain Buddhahood?”

At that time, the Naga girl had a precious jewel, worth the great three-thousand-fold worlds, and presented it to the Buddha. The Buddha immediately accepted it.

The Naga girl said to Prajñākūṭa Bodhisattva and the Venerable Śāriputra: “I offered the precious jewel, and the World-Honored One accepted it. Was this swift or not?”

They answered: “Extremely swift.”

The girl said: “With your spiritual powers, observe me becoming a Buddha, swifter even than this!”

At that moment, the entire assembly saw the Naga girl suddenly transform into a being endowed with Bodhisattva conduct, immediately go to the southern Vimala world, sit upon a jewel lotus, and accomplish equal and right awakening, with thirty-two marks and eighty subtle characteristics, universally expounding the wonderful Dharma for all wandering beings in the ten directions.

Then in the Sahā world, the Bodhisattvas, Śrāvakas, the eightfold assemblies of devas and Nagas, humans, and non-humans, from afar saw the Naga girl become a Buddha, universally expounding the Dharma for the devas and humans assembled at that time, and rejoiced greatly, all bowing in reverence from afar.

Immeasurable wandering beings, hearing the Dharma, understood and awakened, attaining non-retrogression; immeasurable wandering beings received prophecies of the path. The Vimala world shook in six ways; in the Sahā world, three thousand wandering beings came to dwell on the ground of non-retrogression, and three thousand wandering beings generated the bodhicitta and received prophecies. Prajñākūṭa Bodhisattva, Śāriputra, and the entire assembly silently believed and accepted.

← Capítulo anteriorCapítulo siguiente →

Capítulo XIII

Exhortación a sostener

Este capítulo aún está en traducción al castellano; se muestra provisoriamente en inglés.

At that time, Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva Mahāsattva and Mahāpratibhāna Bodhisattva Mahāsattva, together with a retinue of twenty thousand Bodhisattvas, made this vow before the Buddha:

“We only pray that the World-Honored One will have no anxiety. After the Buddha’s extinction, we will uphold, read, recite, and expound this scripture. Wandering beings of the future evil age will have fewer wholesome roots, much arrogant pride, greed for gain and offerings, increasing unwholesome roots, and will be far removed from liberation. Although they are difficult to teach, we will give rise to great power of patience, reading, reciting, holding, expounding, copying, and making various offerings to this scripture, not begrudging physical life.”

Then five hundred arhats in the assembly who had received prophecies addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! We too make our own vows: in other lands, we will widely expound this scripture.”

Furthermore, eight thousand practitioners in training and beyond training who had received prophecies arose from their seats, joined their palms toward the Buddha, and made this vow:

“World-Honored One! We too will widely expound this scripture in other lands. For what reason? In this Sahā world, people are full of flaws and evils, harboring arrogant pride, shallow in virtue, angry, turbid, flattering, and crooked, with ungenuine minds.”

At that time, the Buddha’s maternal aunt, the bhikṣuṇī Mahāprajāpatī, together with six thousand bhikṣuṇīs in training and beyond training, arose from their seats, single-mindedly joined their palms, and gazed upon the venerable countenance without glancing away for an instant.

Then the World-Honored One addressed Gautamī: “Why do you look upon the Tathāgata with a sorrowful expression? Does your mind think that I have not spoken your name to bestow a prophecy of anuttarasamyaksambodhi?

Gautamī! I previously stated generally that all Śrāvakas have already received prophecies. Now if you wish to know your prophecy: in future worlds, within the Dharma of sixty-eight billion Buddhas, you will be a great Dharma teacher, and the six thousand bhikṣuṇīs in training and beyond training will all be Dharma teachers together. In this way, gradually perfecting the Bodhisattva path, you will become a Buddha named Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Tathāgata, Arhat, Samyaksambuddha, Vidyācaraṇasampanna, Sugata, Lokavid, Anuttara, Puruṣadamyasārathi, Śāstā Devamanuṣyāṇām, Buddha, Bhagavān.

Gautamī! This Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Buddha and the six thousand Bodhisattvas will successively bestow prophecies of attaining anuttarasamyaksambodhi.”

Then Rāhula’s mother, the bhikṣuṇī Yaśodharā, thought: “The World-Honored One, in bestowing prophecies, has uniquely omitted my name.”

The Buddha addressed Yaśodharā: “You in future worlds, within the Dharma of hundreds of thousands of millions of Buddhas, will cultivate Bodhisattva conduct as a great Dharma teacher, gradually fulfilling the Buddha path. In the land Bhadra, you will become a Buddha named Daśasahasragauraprabhāsa Tathāgata, Arhat, Samyaksambuddha, Vidyācaraṇasampanna, Sugata, Lokavid, Anuttara, Puruṣadamyasārathi, Śāstā Devamanuṣyāṇām, Buddha, Bhagavān. That Buddha’s lifespan will be immeasurable and incalculable kalpas.”

Then the bhikṣuṇī Mahāprajāpatī and the bhikṣuṇī Yaśodharā, along with their retinues, all rejoiced greatly, attaining what they had never had before, and immediately spoke gāthās before the Buddha:

The World-Honored One, the Guide,

Brings peace to devas and humans,

We, hearing the prophecy,

Have minds completely at peace.

Having spoken these gāthās, the bhikṣuṇīs addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! We too are able widely to proclaim this scripture in lands of other directions.”

At that time, the World-Honored One looked upon eighty billion inconceivable numbers of Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas. All these Bodhisattvas—who were avaivartika, turning the irreversible wheel of Dharma, and had attained dhāraṇīs—arose from their seats, went before the Buddha, single-mindedly joined their palms, and thought:

“If the World-Honored One commands us to uphold and expound this scripture, in accord with the Buddha’s authoritative teaching we will widely proclaim this Dharma.”

Again they thought: “The Buddha is now silent and does not issue a command; what should we do?”

Then the Bodhisattvas, reverently obeying the Buddha’s intent and wishing to fulfill their own original vows, before the Buddha made the lion’s roar and proclaimed a vow:

“World-Honored One! After the Tathāgata’s extinction, traveling throughout worlds of the ten directions, we will cause wandering beings to copy this scripture, receive, uphold, read, recite, explain its meaning, practice it according to the Dharma, and remember it properly—all through the Buddha’s majestic power. We only pray that the World-Honored One, dwelling in other directions, will gaze upon and protect us from afar!”

Immediately the Bodhisattvas simultaneously raised their voices, speaking gāthās:

We only pray you will have no anxiety;

After the Buddha’s extinction,

In a terrifying, evil world,

We will widely expound it.

There are foolish people,

Abusing and reviling with evil mouths,

And even attacking with knives and sticks;

We will endure it all.

Bhikṣus in the evil world,

With perverse wisdom and crooked minds,

Claiming to have attained what they have not attained,

Filled with minds of self-grasping pride.

Or there are those in the araṇya,

Wearing patched robes in quiet leisure,

Claiming they practice the genuine path,

Despising people in the world.

Greedily attached to gain and offerings,

They expound the Dharma to white-robed laity,

Revered by the world,

As if arhats of the six spiritual superknowledges.

These people harbor evil minds,

Constantly thinking of worldly activities,

Taking the false name of araṇya,

Fond of pointing out our faults,

Speaking words like these:

“All these bhikṣus,

Greedy for gain and offerings,

Speak non-Buddhist treatises;

Composing this scripture themselves,

They deceive and confuse worldly people;

To seek fame and renown,

They distinguish in this scripture.”

Constantly amidst the great assembly,

Wishing to defame us,

Addressing kings, ministers,

Brāhmaṇas, householders,

And other assemblies of bhikṣus,

Slandering and speaking evil of us,

Saying: “These are people of false views,

Speaking non-Buddhist treatises.”

Because we revere the Buddha,

We will endure all these evils.

Mocked by them with words:

“You are all Buddhas!”

Derisive words such as these,

We will endure and accept them all.

In the turbid kalpa and evil world,

There are many terrors;

Evil ghosts will enter their bodies,

Reviling and insulting us.

We, revering and trusting the Buddha,

Will put on the armor of patience;

For the sake of expounding this scripture,

We will endure these difficult hardships.

We do not cherish physical life,

Caring only for the unexcelled path;

We in future ages,

Will protect what the Buddha entrusted;

The World-Honored One himself must know!

Evil bhikṣus of the turbid world,

Not knowing the Buddha’s expedient means,

Expounding the Dharma according to what is appropriate,

With evil mouths and scowling faces,

Frequently expelling us,

Sending us far from stūpas and temples.

Evils such as these,

Mindful of the Buddha’s authoritative teaching,

We will endure all these matters.

In all villages and cities,

Wherever there are those who seek the Dharma,

We will all go to where they are,

To expound the Dharma entrusted by the Buddha.

We are envoys of the World-Honored One,

Dwelling amidst the assembly without fear;

We will expound the Dharma skillfully;

May the Buddha dwell peacefully at ease!

Before the World-Honored One,

And the arriving Buddhas of the ten directions,

We make such vows as these;

The Buddha himself knows our hearts.

← Capítulo anteriorCapítulo siguiente →

Capítulo XIV

Prácticas pacíficas

Este capítulo aún está en traducción al castellano; se muestra provisoriamente en inglés.

At that time, Mañjuśrī, Prince of the Dharma, Bodhisattva Mahāsattva, addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! These Bodhisattvas are exceedingly rare. Reverently obeying the Buddha, they have made great vows: in the future evil age, to protect, uphold, read, and expound this Lotus Sūtra. World-Honored One! How can Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas expound this scripture in the future evil age?”

The Buddha addressed Mañjuśrī: “If Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas wish to expound this scripture in the future evil age, they should abide peacefully in four dharmas. First, abiding peacefully in the Bodhisattva’s sphere of practice and sphere of association, they will be able to expound this scripture for wandering beings.

Mañjuśrī! What is named the Bodhisattva Mahāsattva’s sphere of practice? If a Bodhisattva Mahāsattva dwells on the ground of patience, gentle and compliant, not rash or violent, his mind not alarmed; furthermore, having no practice regarding dharmas, contemplating the genuine mark of all dharmas as they are, neither practicing nor discriminating—this is named the Bodhisattva Mahāsattva’s sphere of practice.

What is named the Bodhisattva Mahāsattva’s sphere of association? A Bodhisattva Mahāsattva does not draw close to kings, princes, ministers, or senior officials; he does not draw close to non-Buddhists, brahmacārins, Nirgranthas, and the like, nor to those who compose worldly literature, sing poems, or recite non-Buddhist books, nor to Lokāyatas and anti-Lokāyatas. He does not draw close to dangerous games, mutual boxing, wrestling, or the various illusionary entertainments of acrobats.

He does not draw close to caṇḍālas, nor to those who raise pigs, sheep, chickens, and dogs, or who hunt, fish, or engage in evil livelihoods. If such persons come at times, he expounds the Dharma for them without expectation.

He does not draw close to bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, or upāsikās who seek to be Śrāvakas, nor does he inquire after them. Whether in rooms, walkways, or lecture halls, he does not dwell together with them. If they come at times, he expounds the Dharma according to what is appropriate, without seeking anything.

Mañjuśrī! Furthermore, a Bodhisattva Mahāsattva should not, regarding the bodies of women, seize upon marks that give rise to lustful thoughts to expound the Dharma for them, nor does he delight in looking upon them. If entering the homes of others, he does not speak with young girls, virgins, or widows. He does not draw close to the five kinds of unmanly persons to make them friends. He does not enter the homes of others alone; if due to circumstances he must enter alone, he is solely and single-mindedly mindful of the Buddha.

If expounding the Dharma for women, he does not show his teeth in laughter, nor bare his chest. Even for the sake of Dharma he does not become intimate, how much less for other matters. He does not delight in keeping young disciples, śrāmaṇeras, or children, nor does he delight in sharing the same master with them. He constantly favors seated meditation, dwelling in leisure places to cultivate and collect his mind. Mañjuśrī! This is named the first sphere of association.

Furthermore, a Bodhisattva Mahāsattva contemplates all dharmas as empty, like the genuine mark: unperverted, unmoving, non-regressing, non-turning, like empty space, without inherent nature. All paths of language are severed; unborn, non-emerging, non-arising, nameless, signless, truly non-existent, immeasurable, boundless, unobstructed, unhindered, existing solely through causal conditions, spoken of because born from perversion. Constantly delighting in contemplating such marks of dharmas—this is named the Bodhisattva Mahāsattva’s second sphere of association.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

If there are Bodhisattvas,

In the future evil world,

Without fear in mind,

Wishing to expound this scripture,

They should enter the sphere of practice,

And the sphere of association.

Constantly staying far from kings,

Princes of nations,

Ministers and senior officials,

Those engaged in dangerous games,

Caṇḍālas,

And non-Buddhist brahmacārins.

Neither should they draw close

To those of arrogant pride,

Who are greedily attached to the Small Vehicle,

Scholars of the Three Baskets,

Precept-breaking bhikṣus,

Nominal arhats,

And bhikṣuṇīs

Who are fond of frivolous laughter;

Those deeply attached to the five desires,

Who seek present extinction,

And all upāsikās—

Do not draw close to any of them.

If such persons as these,

Come with wholesome minds

To where the Bodhisattva dwells,

To hear the Buddha path,

The Bodhisattva then,

With a fearless mind,

Without harboring expectations,

Expounds the Dharma for them.

Widows, virgins,

And all unmanly persons,

Do not draw close to them,

To make them intimate friends.

Neither draw close to

Butchers, executioners,

Hunters, and fishermen,

Who kill for profit,

Or sell meat to sustain themselves,

Or peddle female charms;

Persons such as these,

Do not draw close to them.

Dangerous wrestling,

Various entertainments,

And prostitutes—

Do not draw close to any of them.

Do not in a secluded, screened place,

Expound the Dharma for women;

When expounding the Dharma,

Do not jest or laugh.

When entering a village to beg for food,

Take another bhikṣu along;

If there is no other bhikṣu,

Single-mindedly be mindful of the Buddha.

These are named

The sphere of practice and sphere of association;

Relying on these two spheres,

One can expound in peace and joy.

Furthermore, do not practice

Superior, middling, or inferior dharmas,

Conditioned or unconditioned,

Real or unreal dharmas;

Neither discriminate

Whether one is male or female.

Not apprehending all dharmas,

Not knowing and not seeing—

This is named

The Bodhisattva’s sphere of practice.

All dharmas

Are empty and non-existent,

Without permanence,

And without arising or ceasing;

This is named

The wise person’s sphere of association.

Perverted discriminations

Of dharmas as existent or non-existent,

Real or unreal,

Arising or non-arising.

Dwelling in a leisure place,

Cultivating and collecting the mind,

Peacefully abiding unmoving,

Like Mount Sumeru.

Contemplating all dharmas

As having no existence,

Just like empty space,

Without solidity.

Unborn, non-emerging,

Unmoving, non-regressing,

Constantly abiding in one mark;

This is named the sphere of association.

If there are bhikṣus,

After my extinction,

Who enter this sphere of practice

And sphere of association,

When expounding this scripture,

They will have no timidity.

When Bodhisattvas at times

Enter a quiet chamber,

With proper mindfulness,

Contemplating dharmas according to their meaning,

Arising from dhyāna meditation,

For the sake of kings,

Princes, ministers, citizens,

And brāhmaṇas,

Opening, transforming, and proclaiming,

Expounding this scripture,

Their minds will be peaceful and secure,

Free of all timidity.

Mañjuśrī!

This is named the Bodhisattva

Abiding peacefully in the first dharma,

Able in future ages

To expound the Lotus Sūtra.

“Furthermore, Mañjuśrī! After the Tathāgata’s extinction, in the Final Dharma age, if one wishes to expound this scripture, one should abide in peaceful and joyful practice. When speaking with the mouth or reading the scripture, one should not delight in speaking of the faults of persons or scriptures. Neither should one slight other Dharma teachers, nor speak of other people’s virtues or vices, strengths or weaknesses.

Regarding Śrāvakas, one should not name them to speak of their errors and evils, nor name them to praise their virtues, nor give rise to thoughts of resentment and dislike. Because one skillfully cultivates such a peaceful and joyful mind, listeners will not oppose one’s intent; if questioned on difficult points, one does not answer with the Small Vehicle Dharma, but solely through the Great Vehicle explains it for them, causing them to attain all-knowledge.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

The Bodhisattva constantly delights

In expounding the Dharma in peace and security;

Upon a pure ground,

He sets out a seat.

Anointing his body with oil,

Bathing away dirt and grime,

Putting on fresh, clean robes,

Clean both inside and out,

Peacefully seated upon the Dharma seat,

He speaks according to what is asked.

If there are bhikṣus,

Bhikṣuṇīs,

Upāsakas,

Upāsikās,

Kings, princes,

Ministers, and citizens,

With subtle, profound meaning,

He speaks with a gentle countenance.

If there are difficult questions,

He answers according to the meaning,

With causal conditions and parables,

Setting forth distinctions.

Through this expedient means,

He causes them all to generate the resolve,

Gradually increasing,

Entering the Buddha path.

Eliminating lazy thoughts,

And notions of negligence,

Free from all sorrow and affliction,

Expounding the Dharma with a compassionate mind.

Day and night constantly expounding

The teachings of the unexcelled path;

With various causal conditions,

And immeasurable parables,

Opening and revealing to wandering beings,

Causing them all to rejoice.

Clothing, bedding,

Food, drink, and medicine—

Regarding these,

He has no expectation.

Solely with single mind,

Mindful of the causal conditions of expounding the Dharma,

Vowing to accomplish the Buddha path,

And cause the assembly to do likewise;

This is great benefit,

An offering of peace and joy.

After my extinction,

If there are bhikṣus,

Able to expound this

Lotus Sūtra of the Wonderful Dharma,

Their minds free of jealousy and anger,

And all hindrances of affliction,

Free also of sorrow,

And of revilers,

And without fear

Of knives and sticks,

Never expelled,

Because abiding in patience.

The wise person being like this,

Skillfully cultivating his mind,

Can abide in peace and joy,

As I have spoken above.

The merits of this person,

Throughout thousands of billions of kalpas,

Beyond calculation and parables,

Cannot be fully told.

“Furthermore, Mañjuśrī! Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas, in the future Final Dharma age when the Dharma is about to perish, who receive, uphold, read, and recite this scripture, should harbor no mind of envy, flattery, or deceit, nor slight and revile those who study the Buddha path, seeking their strengths and weaknesses.

If there are bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, or upāsikās who seek to be Śrāvakas, who seek to be Pratyekabuddhas, or who seek the Bodhisattva path, one must not distress them, causing them doubt and regret by saying to them: ‘You are very far from the path; you will never attain all-knowledge. For what reason? You are negligent persons, indolent regarding the path.’

Furthermore, one should not engage in discursive play regarding dharmas, nor engage in contention. Toward all wandering beings, one should give rise to the thought of great compassion; toward all Tathāgatas, give rise to the thought of a loving father; toward all Bodhisattvas, give rise to the thought of great teachers; toward the great Bodhisattvas of the ten directions, one should constantly bow and pay homage with deep reverence. Toward all wandering beings, one should expound the Dharma equally, in accordance with the Dharma, neither too much nor too little; even toward those who deeply love the Dharma, one does not speak too much.

Mañjuśrī! This Bodhisattva Mahāsattva, in the future Final Dharma age when the Dharma is about to perish, who accomplishes this third peaceful practice: when he expounds this Dharma, none will be able to disturb or confuse him; he will attain good fellow students to read and recite this scripture with him, and will also attain great assemblies who come to listen and receive it; having heard, they will be able to uphold it; having upheld, they will be able to recite it; having recited, they will be able to expound it; having expounded, they will be able to copy it or cause others to copy it, making offerings to the scriptural scrolls, revering, honoring, and praising them.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

If one wishes to expound this scripture,

One should abandon jealousy, anger, pride,

Flattery, deceit, falsehood, and hypocrisy,

Constantly cultivating upright conduct.

Do not look down upon people,

Nor engage in discursive play regarding dharmas;

Do not cause others doubt and regret,

Saying: “You will not become Buddhas.”

When this Buddha-child expounds the Dharma,

He is constantly gentle and able to endure,

Compassionate toward all,

Not giving rise to indolence in mind.

Great Bodhisattvas of the ten directions,

Who practice the path out of pity for beings,

One should generate a reverent mind toward them:

“These are my great masters.”

Toward the Buddhas, the World-Honored Ones,

Give rise to the thought of unexcelled fathers;

Shattering minds of haughty pride,

Expounding the Dharma without hindrance.

The third dharma is like this;

The wise person should guard it.

Single-mindedly in peaceful practice,

He is revered by immeasurable multitudes.

“Furthermore, Mañjuśrī! Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas, in the future Final Dharma age when the Dharma is about to perish, who uphold this Lotus Sūtra, should give rise to a mind of great love toward those at home and those who have left home, and give rise to a mind of great compassion toward those who are not Bodhisattvas, thinking:

‘Such persons suffer a great loss. The Tathāgata, through expedient means, expounds the Dharma according to what is appropriate, yet they do not hear, do not know, do not perceive, do not ask, do not believe, and do not understand. Although these persons do not ask, do not believe, and do not understand this scripture, when I attain anuttarasamyaksambodhi, wherever they may be, through spiritual power and wisdom power I will guide them, causing them to abide in this Dharma.’

Mañjuśrī! This Bodhisattva Mahāsattva, after the Tathāgata’s extinction, who accomplishes this fourth dharma: when he expounds this Dharma, he will be free of faults, and will constantly receive offerings, reverence, honor, and praise from bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, upāsikās, kings, princes, ministers, citizens, brāhmaṇas, and householders. The devas of open space, for the sake of hearing the Dharma, will also constantly follow and attend upon him.

If he is in villages, cities, or leisure forests, and people come wishing to pose difficult questions, the devas day and night will constantly guard and protect him for the sake of the Dharma, causing all listeners to attain joy. For what reason? Because this scripture is protected by the spiritual power of all Buddhas of the past, future, and present. Mañjuśrī! In immeasurable lands, one cannot even hear the name of this Lotus Sūtra, how much less behold, receive, uphold, read, and recite it!

Mañjuśrī! Suppose a powerful wheel-turning sage king wishes to subdue various lands by force, but petty kings do not obey his commands. The wheel-turning king mobilizes various troops to punish them. The king, seeing those among his soldiers who have achieved merit in battle, rejoices greatly and bestows rewards according to their merit: giving fields, houses, villages, and cities; or giving garments and personal ornaments; or giving various treasures: gold, silver, lapis lazuli, mother-of-pearl, agate, coral, amber, elephants, horses, carriages, and servants. Only the bright jewel in his topknot does he not give away.

For what reason? Only on the crown of the king’s head is there this single jewel; if he were to give it away, the king’s attendants would certainly be greatly startled and amazed.

Mañjuśrī! The Tathāgata is also like this. Through the power of dhyāna meditation and wisdom, he attains the Dharma realm and reigns over the three realms, yet the Mara kings refuse to submit. The Tathāgata’s wise and holy generals battle with them; when they achieve merit, his heart also rejoices.

Amidst the fourfold assembly, he expounds various scriptures for them, causing their hearts to delight, bestowing upon them dhyāna meditation, liberation, uncontaminated faculties and powers, and the wealth of all dharmas; he also bestows upon them the city of nirvāṇa, telling them they have attained extinction, guiding their minds and causing them all to rejoice; yet he does not expound this Lotus Sūtra for them.

Mañjuśrī! Just as that wheel-turning king, seeing those among his troops who have achieved great merit, rejoices exceedingly, and with this jewel difficult to believe, which has long resided in his topknot and was never recklessly given to anyone, now gives it away:

The Tathāgata is also like this. Within the three realms, he is the Great King of Dharma, teaching and transforming all wandering beings through the Dharma. Seeing the army of the wise and holy battle with the skandha-maras, affliction-maras, and death-maras, achieving great merit, extinguishing the three poisons, escaping the three realms, and shattering the net of Maras, the Tathāgata also rejoices greatly.

This Lotus Sūtra, able to cause wandering beings to arrive at all-knowledge, is resented and difficult to believe in all the world; not having been spoken before, he now expounds it. Mañjuśrī! This Lotus Sūtra is the foremost teaching of the Tathāgatas, the most profound among all teachings, bestowed at the very end, just like that powerful king who long guarded the bright jewel, and now finally bestows it.

Mañjuśrī! This Lotus Sūtra is the secret treasury of all Buddhas, the Tathāgatas, standing supreme among all scriptures. Guarded through the long night, it has not been recklessly proclaimed; only beginning today is it set forth and expounded for all of you.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

Constantly practicing patience,

Pitying all beings,

Only then can one proclaim

The scripture praised by the Buddhas.

In the future Final Dharma age,

Those who uphold this scripture,

Toward those at home, those who have left home,

And those who are not Bodhisattvas,

Should give rise to love and compassion:

“These who do not hear,

And do not believe this scripture,

Suffer a great loss.

When I attain the Buddha path,

Through various expedient means,

I will expound this Dharma for them,

Causing them to abide within it.”

Just like a powerful

Wheel-turning king,

When troops have merit in battle,

Bestowing various gifts:

Elephants, horses, carriages,

Personal ornaments,

Fields, houses,

Villages, and cities;

Or giving clothing,

Various treasures,

Servants, and wealth,

Joyfully bestowing them.

If there is a heroic warrior,

Able to perform difficult feats,

The king unties from his topknot

The bright jewel to bestow upon him.

The Tathāgata is also like this;

As King of all dharmas,

With the great power of patience,

And treasury of wisdom,

Through great compassion,

Transforms the world according to Dharma.

Seeing all humans

Undergoing various sufferings,

Wishing to seek liberation,

Battling with the Maras.

For the sake of these wandering beings,

He speaks various dharmas;

Through great expedient means,

Expounding these scriptures.

Having known that wandering beings

Have attained their strength,

At the very end,

He expounds this Lotus for them,

Just as the king unties his topknot,

To bestow the bright jewel.

This scripture is supreme,

Foremost among all scriptures;

I have constantly guarded it,

Not recklessly revealing it;

Now is precisely the time

To expound it for you all.

After my extinction,

Those who seek the Buddha path,

Wishing to attain peace and security

To proclaim this scripture,

Should draw close to

Such four dharmas as these.

Those who read this scripture

Will constantly be free of sorrow and affliction,

Free also of illness and pain,

Their complexions fresh and bright;

Not born into poverty,

Baseness, or ugliness.

Wandering beings will delight in seeing them,

Admiring them as worthy sages;

Sons of devas

Will serve as their attendants.

Knives and sticks will not strike them,

Poisons cannot harm them;

If someone reviles them,

Their mouths will be shut.

They will travel without fear,

Like lion kings;

The light of their wisdom,

Like the shining of the sun.

If in dreams,

They will see only wonderful matters:

Beholding the Tathāgatas,

Seated upon lion seats,

Surrounded by assemblies of bhikṣus,

Expounding the Dharma.

Also seeing Nagas, spirits,

And asuras,

Equal to the sands of the Ganges,

Reverently joining palms;

Seeing their own bodies,

Expounding the Dharma for them.

Again beholding the Buddhas,

Their bodies golden in color,

Emitting immeasurable light,

Illuminating all,

With Brahma-sounds,

Expounding all dharmas.

The Buddha expounds the unexcelled Dharma

For the fourfold assembly;

They see themselves in their midst,

Joining palms and praising the Buddha,

Hearing the Dharma with joy,

Making offerings,

Attaining dhāraṇī,

Realizing irreversible wisdom.

The Buddha, knowing their minds

Have entered deeply into the Buddha path,

Immediately bestows a prophecy

Of accomplishing the most right awakening:

“You, good son!

In a future age,

Will attain immeasurable wisdom,

The great path of the Buddha.

Your land will be adorned and pure,

Vast and incomparable;

There will also be a fourfold assembly,

Joining palms to listen to the Dharma.”

They also see themselves

In mountain forests,

Cultivating wholesome dharmas,

Realizing the genuine mark,

Deeply entering dhyāna meditation,

Beholding the Buddhas of the ten directions.

The bodies of the Buddhas are golden,

Adorned with marks of a hundred merits;

Hearing the Dharma and speaking it to others,

They constantly have such good dreams.

Again dreaming of being a king,

Abandoning palaces and attendants,

And superior pleasures of the five desires,

Going to the place of awakening.

Beneath the Bodhi tree,

Seated upon a lion seat,

Seeking the path for seven days,

Attaining the wisdom of the Buddhas.

Having accomplished the unexcelled path,

Arising to turn the wheel of Dharma,

Expounding the Dharma for the fourfold assembly

Throughout thousands of billions of kalpas,

Expounding the uncontaminated, wondrous Dharma,

Liberating immeasurable wandering beings.

Afterward entering nirvāṇa,

As smoke vanishes and a lamp goes out.

If in the future evil world,

One expounds this foremost Dharma,

That person will attain great benefit,

Such virtues and merits as spoken above.

← Capítulo anteriorCapítulo siguiente →

Capítulo XV

La emergencia de la tierra

Este capítulo aún está en traducción al castellano; se muestra provisoriamente en inglés.

At that time, the Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas who had arrived from other lands, exceeding the sands of eight Ganges rivers, arose in the great assembly, joined their palms, bowed, and addressed the Buddha:

“World-Honored One! If you permit us, after the Buddha’s extinction, in this Sahā world, we will exert diligence and vigor, protecting, upholding, reading, reciting, copying, and making offerings to this scripture, and will widely expound it in this land.”

Then the Buddha addressed the assembly of Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas: “Cease, Good children! There is no need for you to protect and uphold this scripture. For what reason? In my Sahā world, there are already Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas equal to the sands of sixty thousand Ganges rivers, each Bodhisattva having retinues equal to the sands of sixty thousand Ganges rivers. These persons will be able, after my extinction, to protect, uphold, read, recite, and widely expound this scripture.”

When the Buddha spoke this, the ground of the great three-thousand-fold worlds of the Sahā world shook and cracked open, and from within it, immeasurable thousands of billions of Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas simultaneously welled forth.

All these Bodhisattvas had golden bodies, thirty-two marks, and immeasurable light; they had previously dwelled below this Sahā world, residing in open space within this realm. These Bodhisattvas, hearing the sound spoken by Śākyamuni Buddha, came forth from below.

Each Bodhisattva was a leader of a great assembly, each leading retinues equal to the sands of sixty thousand Ganges rivers; how much more so those who led retinues equal to fifty thousand, forty thousand, thirty thousand, twenty thousand, or ten thousand Ganges sands; how much more so those with retinues down to one Ganges sand, half a Ganges sand, a quarter, or down to one part in tens of billions of inconceivable numbers; how much more so those with retinues of tens of billions of inconceivable numbers; how much more so those with billions of retinues; how much more so those with tens of millions, millions, or down to ten thousand; how much more so those with one thousand, one hundred, or down to ten; how much more so those who led five, four, three, two, or one disciple; how much more so those who were solitary, delighting in isolated practice! Comparisons such as these are immeasurable, boundless, beyond calculation or parables to know.

These Bodhisattvas, having emerged from the earth, each went up into open space to the wonderful seven-jeweled stūpa, to where Prabhūtaratna Tathāgata and Śākyamuni Buddha were. Having arrived, they bowed with heads touching the feet of the two World-Honored Ones; they also went to the Buddhas seated upon lion seats beneath the jewel trees, bowed, circumambulated them three times to the right, joined their palms reverently, and praised them with various Bodhisattva praises, then stood on one side, joyfully gazing upon the two World-Honored Ones.

From the time these Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas first welled forth, praising the Buddhas with various Bodhisattva praises, a span of fifty minor kalpas elapsed. During this time, Śākyamuni Buddha sat in silence, and the fourfold assembly also sat in silence for fifty minor kalpas; through the Buddha’s spiritual power, the great assembly perceived it as half a day.

At that time, the fourfold assembly, also through the Buddha’s spiritual power, saw the Bodhisattvas filling the open space of immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of lands. Among this assembly of Bodhisattvas, there were four guides: the first named Viśiṣṭacāritra, the second named Anantacāritra, the third named Viśuddhacāritra, and the fourth named Supratiṣṭhitacāritra.

These four Bodhisattvas were the foremost leaders and guiding teachers among the multitude. Before the great assembly, each joined palms, observed Śākyamuni Buddha, and inquired: “World-Honored One! Are you free of sickness and affliction, practicing in peace and joy? Are those to be liberated easy to teach? They do not cause the World-Honored One to grow weary, do they?”

Then the four great Bodhisattvas spoke gāthās:

Is the World-Honored One peaceful and joyful,

Free of sickness and affliction?

In teaching and transforming wandering beings,

Are you free from weariness?

And are all the wandering beings

Easy to transform?

They do not cause the World-Honored One

To give rise to fatigue, do they?

Then the World-Honored One, amidst the great assembly of Bodhisattvas, spoke these words: “Thus it is, thus it is, Good children! The Tathāgata is peaceful and joyful, free of sickness and affliction. Wandering beings are easy to transform, and I have no fatigue.

For what reason? These wandering beings, life after life, have constantly received my transformation, and have also made offerings and shown reverence under past Buddhas, planting roots of virtue. When these wandering beings first saw my body and heard what I expounded, they all immediately believed, accepted, and entered the Tathāgata’s wisdom—except for those who previously practiced and studied the Small Vehicle. Such persons as these, I now also cause to hear this scripture and enter the Buddha’s wisdom.”

Then the great Bodhisattvas spoke gāthās:

Excellent, excellent!

Great Hero, World-Honored One!

All these wandering beings

Are easy to transform.

Able to inquire into the Buddhas’

Profound wisdom,

Hearing it, they believe and practice;

We rejoice in accord!

Then the World-Honored One praised the leading great Bodhisattvas: “Excellent, excellent, Good children! You are able to give rise to a mind of rejoicing in accord with the Tathāgata.”

At that time, Maitreya Bodhisattva and the assembly of Bodhisattvas equal to the sands of eight Ganges rivers all had this thought: “From the past until now, we have never seen or heard of such an assembly of great Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas welling forth from the earth, standing before the World-Honored One, joining palms, making offerings, and inquiring after the Tathāgata.”

Then Maitreya Bodhisattva Mahāsattva, knowing the thoughts in the minds of the Bodhisattvas equal to the sands of eight Ganges rivers, and wishing to resolve his own doubt, joined his palms toward the Buddha and inquired in gāthās:

Immeasurable thousands of billions,

A great assembly of Bodhisattvas,

Never seen in the past;

We pray the Honored Among Two-Legged Beings will speak:

From where have they come?

By what causal condition have they assembled?

Huge bodies, great spiritual powers,

Wisdom inconceivable,

Their resolve and mindfulness firm,

Possessing great power of patience,

Delighted in by wandering beings—

From where have they come?

Each of these Bodhisattvas,

Leading retinues,

Their numbers having no measure,

Like the sands of the Ganges.

Some great Bodhisattvas,

Lead sixty thousand Ganges sands;

Such great multitudes as these,

Single-mindedly seek the Buddha path.

These great masters,

Sixty thousand Ganges sands,

Have come together to make offerings to the Buddha,

And to protect and uphold this scripture.

Those leading fifty thousand Ganges sands,

Their numbers exceed even these.

Forty thousand and thirty thousand,

Twenty thousand down to ten thousand,

One thousand, one hundred,

Down to one Ganges sand,

Half, a third, or a quarter,

Down to one part in tens of billions,

Tens of billions of inconceivable numbers,

Tens of billions of disciples,

Down to half a billion,

Their numbers surpass even these.

From one million to ten thousand,

One thousand and one hundred,

Fifty and ten,

Down to three, two, or one,

Solitary without retinues,

Delighting in solitary living,

Coming together to where the Buddha is,

Their numbers exceed even these.

Such great assemblies as these,

If someone were to count them with tally rods,

Throughout kalpas like Ganges sands,

They still could not fully know them.

All these great, majestic,

Vigorous Bodhisattva assemblies—

Who expounded the Dharma for them,

Teaching, transforming, and accomplishing them?

From whom did they first generate the resolve?

What Buddha-Dharma do they praise?

Whose scriptures do they uphold and practice?

What Buddha path do they cultivate?

Such Bodhisattvas as these,

With spiritual powers and great wisdom strength,

When the earth in the four directions cracked open,

All welled forth from within it.

World-Honored One, from the past,

I have never seen such a thing;

Pray speak of where they come from,

And the names of their lands.

I constantly travel throughout lands,

Yet have never seen this assembly;

Among this assembly,

I do not recognize a single person;

Suddenly emerging from the earth,

Pray speak of their causal conditions.

Now in this great assembly,

Immeasurable hundreds of thousands of billions,

All these Bodhisattvas,

Wish to understand this matter.

Regarding these Bodhisattva assemblies,

The root and branch causal conditions,

World-Honored One of immeasurable virtue,

We only pray you will resolve the assembly’s doubts!

At that time, the emanation Buddhas of Śākyamuni Buddha, arriving from immeasurable thousands of billions of lands in other directions, sat cross-legged upon lion seats beneath jewel trees in the eight directions.

The attendants of those Buddhas each saw this great assembly of Bodhisattvas in the four directions of the great three-thousand-fold worlds, welling forth from the earth and residing in open space. Each addressed their Buddha: “World-Honored One! From where has this immeasurable, boundless incalculable assembly of Bodhisattvas come?”

Then the Buddhas each addressed their attendants: “Good children! Wait an instant. There is a Bodhisattva Mahāsattva named Maitreya, upon whom Śākyamuni Buddha conferred a prophecy that he will next become a Buddha. He has already inquired of this matter; the Buddha is now answering it, and through this you will hear it for yourselves.”

Then Śākyamuni Buddha addressed Maitreya Bodhisattva: “Excellent, excellent, Ajita! That you are able to question the Buddha on such a great matter. You should all join together with one mind, put on the armor of vigor, and generate a firm resolve; the Tathāgata now wishes to reveal and proclaim the wisdom of the Buddhas, the sovereign spiritual powers of the Buddhas, the lion-like swift stride of the Buddhas, and the majestic, fierce, great power of the Buddhas.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

Be vigorous with one mind;

I wish to speak of this matter.

Do not harbor doubt or regret;

The Buddha’s wisdom is inconceivable.

Now bring forth the power of faith,

Abiding in patient goodness;

Dharmas never heard in the past,

You will now hear them all.

I now comfort you;

Do not harbor doubt or fear.

The Buddha has no ungenuine words;

His wisdom is immeasurable.

The foremost Dharma attained,

Is profound and beyond discrimination;

Such as this I will now expound;

Listen with one mind!

The World-Honored One, having spoken these gāthās, addressed Maitreya Bodhisattva: “I now announce to you all in this great assembly. Ajita! These great Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas, immeasurable, countless and incalculable, who welled forth from the earth, whom you have never seen in the past—after I attained anuttarasamyaksambodhi in this Sahā world, I taught, transformed, and guided these Bodhisattvas, subdued their minds, and caused them to generate the resolve for the path.

All these Bodhisattvas dwell below this Sahā world, residing in open space within this realm. Regarding all scriptures, they read, recite, penetrate fluently, contemplate, distinguish, and properly remember them.

Ajita! These Good children do not delight in speaking much amidst multitudes; they constantly delight in quiet places, diligently practicing vigor without resting; neither do they dwell relying on humans or devas. Constantly delighting in deep wisdom, they are free of hindrances, and constantly delight in the Dharma of the Buddhas, single-mindedly vigorous, seeking unexcelled wisdom.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

Ajita, you should know!

All these great Bodhisattvas,

Throughout countless kalpas,

Have cultivated the Buddha’s wisdom;

All were transformed by me,
